# Project compliance and acceptance

Project: Sbarbase, an open-source operations layer using original Supabase services. Owner: repository maintainer. Product direction: tested multi-project self-hosting with secure configuration and recovery.

Publication baseline: `6c112c35b89b4271d5c007e427daf5cee4fe25c3`. Documentation review date: 2026-10-04. Earlier assessments beginning at `b88e2f1343e16a0210e3e4bcbd3429e3e8c563c3` remain historical, with source-bound evidence below. No product release approval is established. [PROJECT_GOAL.md](PROJECT_GOAL.md) records the owner constraints: Linux Docker containers, open source and no private workstation dependency.

Declared package version: 0.2.0, a development snapshot. Historical 0.1.0 release evidence does not establish a signed 0.2.0 release. Runtime images remain those in the existing lock files. Later scoped runtime and offline evidence is recorded below and in the ledger; it does not transfer to another source revision.

## Sources and applicability

| Source | Reviewed | Version/effective scope | Type | Applies because | Prohibited or excluded action |

| [Self-hosting](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/self-hosting) | 2026-10-03 | Current documentation | Technical constraint | Upstream service and management scope | Claiming complete managed-platform equivalence without implementation/evidence |
| [Docker](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/self-hosting/docker) | 2026-10-03 | Selected self-hosted/v0.8.2, full source identity recorded | Technical reference | Deployment baseline | Treating image packaging as proof for every host |
| [Updating](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/self-hosting/updating) | 2026-10-03 | Current version-aware update tooling | Technical constraint/recommendation | Config merging and upstream releases | Treating configuration backup as database/object recovery |
| [Database backups](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/platform/backups) | 2026-10-03 | Current cloud backup behavior | Technical constraint | Migration and recovery completeness | Claiming a database dump restores object contents |
| [PostgreSQL maintenance notice](https://supabase.com/changelog/postgres-15-19-17-11-breaking-changes) | 2026-10-03 | Published 2026-09-25 | Security applicability review | Current Postgres pin and extension usage need comparison | Assuming unaffected or substituting an untested image |
| [MCP](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/self-hosting/enable-mcp) | 2026-10-03 | Current documented integration | Technical reference | Agent workflow planning | Bypassing environment scope or permissions |
| Repository LICENSE, dependency licenses | 2026-10-03 | Exact distributed revisions still require inventory | Binding license obligations | Redistribution | Removing required notices or assuming one license covers every component |
| [Ubuntu package snapshots](https://snapshot.ubuntu.com/) | 2026-10-03 | Public 20261002T000000Z package snapshot | Technical reference | Reproducible verification image package resolution | Replacing frozen packages with silently mutable repositories |
| [Project goal](PROJECT_GOAL.md) and repository instructions | 2026-10-03 | Current owner rules | Owner acceptance criteria | Open-source distribution and verification | Private workstation dependencies, claims without current scoped proof, secret disclosure, long dashes |

Supabase branding, distribution terms, component licenses and jurisdictional obligations need exact-version review before a release or commercial service. Neither cloud certifications nor a cloud provider's legal commitments automatically apply to this self-host product. No jurisdiction, customer data category or certification target is established, so legal applicability remains unresolved rather than assumed satisfied.

## Acceptance command

The current scoped command, from the repository root, is:

```bash
sh deploy/verify/run.sh
```

It builds a disposable Linux image from distributed source, installs locked Bun dependencies during image build, then runs offline without bind mounts, Docker socket access or network. It records plan integrity, Python unit tests, Bun tests, UI type checking, UI build and Python compilation as separate stages. A failed, empty or skipped unit suite returns nonzero. Evidence is retained under the unique directory printed by the wrapper in `.lab/portable-verification/`.

`deploy/check_plan.py` checks named planning artifacts, local file targets, forbidden characters, whitespace, the G0-G12 ledger and source-reference checksums. Its negative tests confirm refusal for missing artifacts, invalid references and incomplete claimed completion. It is a planning integrity check, not a runtime gate.

The first offline image run on 2026-10-03 failed: Python reported 12 failures, 2 errors and 2 skips across 1,054 tests; Bun passed 295 tests; UI type checking passed; UI build terminated with SIGABRT; compilation passed. These failures remain recorded in the gauntlet ledger. Subsequent changes isolate host-dependent unit fixtures instead of excluding tests. The next full run checked 1,062 Python tests with one remaining fixture failure and no skips; all other stages passed, including UI build after Docker init reaping. A final frozen-image run passed all six mechanical source stages: 1,062 Python tests and 295 Bun tests, with no skips. The independent review accepted that source-only specification. The ledger retains its exact source/image/log hashes. This historical snapshot predates the evidence-summary update; subsequent planning/document checks cover the changed summaries.

The prior frozen build emitted a chunk larger than 500 kB and two module-level `use client` diagnostics from lucide-react. The current scoped repair preserves client directives in emitted chunks, splits React/Lucide from the app, and passes focused build/type checks without those warnings. A fresh offline full run is required for current-source evidence. Unknown warning formats and broader runtime/release acceptance remain unresolved.

This command is a source verification slice. It does not run Supabase, prove portability on independent hosts or establish release acceptance. Warning inventory, runtime fixtures and stale or mismatched release evidence rejection remain incomplete. The existing `deploy/server-acceptance.sh` is a server rehearsal entry point, not a complete compliance gate. G0 and SB-01 require a comprehensive executable gate that rejects warnings, missing evidence, stale evidence and incomplete required cases before product release acceptance.

## Release workflow and limits

Research, implement within the approved scope, run affected checks, repair failures, refresh evidence and read back artifacts. Before release, run the comprehensive gate against the exact revision and independently verify the deployed artifact. Publishing or paid infrastructure actions require the applicable authorization; none was performed here.

Research completion is distinct from production acceptance. Required unrun, failed, unknown or stale evidence prevents release acceptance. Non-applicable categories require a factual rationale: the current slice adds source verification tooling and test fixtures, with no payment flow, new product asset or production deployment. Runtime, image dependency and distribution checks remain applicable where indicated above.

## Current source diagnostics slice

Before implementation, freeze this scoped acceptance contract: zero-exit UI build/typecheck or Python compilation with recognized warning/error diagnostics must fail source acceptance, retain original command exit status and exact log line references, and still execute later stages. Recognize Vite/Rolldown codes, Vite reporter warning markers, ordinary warning/error prefixes and Python compiler warning classes. ANSI formatting cannot hide a diagnostic. Test output intentionally exercising warning/error handling is not a build diagnostic. Unknown diagnostic formats remain a documented limitation requiring log review, not proof of a complete release gate.

UI warning repair must preserve actual browser behavior, tree shaking and dependency directives. Do not mute warnings, increase the 500 kB limit or edit upstream dependencies. Each emitted JavaScript chunk must remain below the existing limit. Review official [Rolldown directives](https://rolldown.rs/in-depth/directives) and [code splitting](https://rolldown.rs/reference/OutputOptions.codeSplitting), reviewed 2026-10-03. This slice does not grant G0 or runtime/release acceptance.

## Missing-root safety slice

The predeclared Docker portability contract already required refusal when the selected persistent-data root is unavailable. A minimal runtime fix now prevents a missing path or regular file from being substituted with its existing parent. Existing valid directories keep exact-target discovery, partition/subvolume and device-number handling. The resource-policy suite passed all 39 tests, including two red-first cases and a custom directory with spaces. IO flags and ceilings remain mandatory. Full daemon endpoint, root/mount validation and independent kernel enforcement remain required under G1.

Current focused verification: `PYTHONPATH=lab /usr/bin/python3 -m unittest lab.test_verify_diagnostics lab.test_portable_verify lab.test_plan_acceptance lab.test_resource_policy` passed 54 tests before terminal-control cases were added; the extended focused diagnostics suite now passes six tests. An earlier invocation without `PYTHONPATH=lab` failed one module import and was corrected, not treated as a product failure or suppressed. `bun test tests/ui-client-boundaries.test.ts` passed one semantic bundle test with seven assertions, including retained unrelated diagnostics.

Independent diagnostics review found an actual false pass from an OSC hyperlink around a zero-exit warning. The corrected normalizer covers terminated OSC/control strings, CSI in seven/eight-bit forms and ordinary ESC sequences, preserving source-log newline counts. Unclosed or residual control codes refuse rather than hide diagnostics. Six red-first terminal-control regressions now pass; the independent real-child probe preserves raw bytes, actual child exit zero, failed acceptance, and later-stage execution. Reference: [XTerm control sequences](https://invisible-island.net/xterm/ctlseqs/ctlseqs.html), patch 411 dated 2026-08-23, reviewed 2026-10-03. This is log classification, not terminal-screen emulation.

Final diagnostics/safety source snapshot: `43c54fa78720df10b6d128696f42f7503622ff284b165a2a3b77919facbc38cb`, image `sha256:23515dc9984401e5b66ae4e21504f83a5162aac80dae0b065409ba76a4ca68e7`. Six stages passed, including 1,071 Python and 296 Bun tests, no skipped suites and no recognized UI/compiler diagnostics. The independent fresh-context reviewer accepted the scoped diagnostics, emitted client-bundle and missing-root specifications. JavaScript chunks measured 313,205, 218,849 and 12,912 bytes. These observations precede this evidence-summary update; planning/document checks are rerun on changed summaries. Full G0/G1, real services, independent host and release acceptance remain unproven. The ledger preserves unsuccessful iterations rather than replacing them with this result.

## Local Docker configuration slice

G1 configuration work retains the predeclared requirement: use the selected local Linux daemon and its exact persistent root, verify the running controller's own full container identity and bind mounts, refuse mismatches before provisioning, and preserve all IO ceilings. Custom paths must not depend on this workstation. Rootless, Docker Desktop, remote daemons and independent kernel enforcement remain unvalidated. An unconfigured native installation retains legacy behavior; declaring a profile enables strict validation.

Additional startup contract: a validator baked into the controller image runs before the checkout's upgrade guard. A default-root historical checkout can reach recovery without depending on a new checkout module. Custom roots require the runtime's explicit local-v1 capability marker before and after the guard, so rollback cannot silently select the old hard-coded root. This is a scoped compatibility gate, not complete upgrade or recovery acceptance.

Official references reviewed 2026-10-03: [Docker daemon data root](https://docs.docker.com/engine/daemon/), [Docker CLI environment precedence](https://docs.docker.com/reference/cli/docker/), [Compose bind mounts and labels](https://docs.docker.com/reference/compose-file/services/), [Compose cgroup namespace](https://docs.docker.com/reference/compose-file/services/#cgroup). The selected Compose profile requires host cgroup namespaces and Compose 2.15 or later. Tests cover configuration, exact identity and refusal ordering. A disposable read-only API probe may cover the available daemon only; it cannot establish independent-host portability or measured IO enforcement.

A red-first preflight test initially lacked isolation of its mutating runner. It entered the installation path and created or adjusted private directory metadata, then admission refused an unclassified container before any runtime stop or start. The diagnostic stack and a read-only Docker events query for that interval confirmed no Docker lifecycle events. No secret contents were read. The corrected test mocks the mutating runner with an assertion failure and the implementation blocks before inventory, locks, pulls or provisioning. This unsuccessful test is retained as an isolation defect, not acceptance evidence.

Independent local-profile review: SPEC MET for configuration and refusal ordering only. The reviewer reproduced an additional image-pull bypass, then confirmed the CLI boundary and helper now refuse before image inventory or pulls. Independent adversarial identity/mount/cache probes and actual startup script fixtures passed, including custom-root refusal after a guard selects an older runtime. The marker declares compatibility; it cannot prove arbitrary runtime behavior.

Frozen source `e4aef4cbea92698a31609837bd9b94da2d8377ea163a85eee692fc9baae67bd9`, image `sha256:371d5d7f3097ffd05ea513339975c9350bce85b81a49840529a608fdabbd416e`: all six offline stages passed, with 1,098 Python and 296 Bun tests and no skipped suites or recognized UI/compiler diagnostics. This snapshot predates the new probe Dockerfile, Docker guide and evidence summaries; runtime artifacts are unchanged. The new thin probe image built without warnings after an earlier invalid-default-argument warning was repaired. Its layers retain the same frozen source and a pinned public Docker CLI. Four disposable live cases passed: matching profile succeeds; wrong root source, read-only socket and private cgroup namespace refuse. Canonical Compose labels were supplied manually, so this is not a complete Compose startup trial. No Supabase services were started, and only owned probe containers/images were removed. Exact hashes and unsuccessful build iterations remain in the ledger. Full G1 and release acceptance remain unproven.

## Frozen upstream bundle prerequisite

Before implementation, declare this G0/G2 prerequisite contract: re-fetch the named public upstream commit, inventory every file in its docker subtree with path, mode and SHA-256, retain all core and optional Compose image references, and bind each resolved Linux platform to immutable image manifest/config digests. Missing, ambiguous, substituted or unresolved mandatory inputs refuse acceptance; platform absence is explicit and does not become a passing runtime profile. Never execute upstream setup scripts, pull service images or start services to collect metadata. A valid source packet is a prerequisite for comparative fixtures, not G2 service or security acceptance. Current tags cannot retroactively redefine the frozen benchmark. Runtime image upgrades remain separate reviewed migration work.

Affected sources refreshed 2026-10-03: [official Docker installation](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/self-hosting/docker), still naming self-hosted/v0.8.2 in its manual example, and [PostgreSQL maintenance notice](https://supabase.com/changelog/postgres-15-19-17-11-breaking-changes). The candidate currently records Supabase Postgres 17.6.1.166; exact exposure, upstream fixes and migration requirements need applicability review. The selected baseline being official does not itself imply it is a current safe production bundle. Repository-owned metadata verification must run with public dependencies from the distributed Linux verification image; contributor investigation may use host tools.

Current build audit found the runtime Docker ignore file still excluded the newly baked profile validator. Source tests had passed because their Dockerfile uses a separate broader public-source context. Correct the runtime context allowlist and build the actual runtime Dockerfile before treating this packaging path as verified. This invalidates any interpretation of the earlier configuration verdict as proof of a successful controller image build; it did not claim that scope. Runtime base image and package reproducibility remain separate G0 work.

The actual runtime Dockerfile now builds with the corrected allowlist. A disposable network-disabled, unprivileged inspection container confirmed the baked validator and executable startup script have exactly the current source-file hashes. Image `sha256:d43dc217c5298bbc90c331465ed41c8e8530033ed0b0a6290d3147a2c9456ee5`; local build/file evidence is `.lab/runtime-build-g1-20261003-402456.*`. This proves file packaging only, not supervisor startup, Supabase operation, reproducible runtime package resolution or independent-host support. Only that owned inspection container and image were removed.

The [candidate image applicability review](docs/engineering/reviews/2026-10-03-image-bundle-applicability.md) records all nine public OCI indexes and 18 hash-verified Linux platform manifest/configuration relations. The available containerd image store reports index-shaped Docker image IDs; forcing equality to a configuration digest would falsely reject it. Classic-store behavior remains untested. Runtime use of repository-qualified immutable references and portable identity reconciliation require implementation and cross-store trials. PostgreSQL package safety and database-specific maintenance applicability remain unknown until exact binary/extension inventory and restored-copy drills are obtained.

The frozen upstream packet is now a retained partial artifact: 64 source files, 14 Compose models, 23 image references, 19 hash-verified root manifests and 38 platform records. The independent reviewer returned SPEC NOT MET for packet acceptance. Missing inbucket, Studio, Supavisor and Vector proof remains after Docker Hub 429 responses; the upstream development file's obsolete-version warning and build-only Studio context remain unresolved. Initial whole-commit fetch timed out; filtered public checkout succeeded. The collector's original auxiliary-YAML classification gap was reproduced independently and fixed with exact pinned path/checksum exceptions. No error or image was silently omitted. Config digests come from verified manifest descriptors and platform values from registry inspection, not retained raw config bytes.

Actual Docker-only `sh deploy/verify/reference-run.sh verify` exited 1 and wrote refused evidence under `.lab/reference-verification/reference-20261003T022128Z-903992-0`. It avoided external calls because mandatory packet proof was already missing. The reviewer confirmed nonroot execution, no mounts/socket/forwarded credentials, matching image identities and owned-resource cleanup. Review reused an earlier critic agent and was not a fresh-context or anonymous comparison. No packet, G0 or G2 acceptance is granted. An earlier wrapper freeze used an older source snapshot and also exited 1; it remains historical evidence, not current proof.

Final source run `.lab/portable-verification/run-20261003T022129Z-904232-0` passed all six stages with 1,114 Python and 296 Bun tests, zero skips and no recognized UI/compiler diagnostics. Its exact source/image/log hashes are in the ledger. This snapshot precedes the evidence-summary edits, which receive focused planning/document checks separately. Source acceptance can pass while required metadata/runtime acceptance remains explicitly refused. Actual service contracts, independent host/store behavior and distribution/security requirements remain unproven.

## Immutable runtime build input slice

Before implementation, declare the controller-image contract: Ubuntu, Bun and Docker CLI image sources use complete public multiarchitecture SHA-256 references; runtime package selection uses the same public Ubuntu snapshot and checksum-pinned CA bootstrap as source verification; repository update errors fail instead of proceeding with partial package indexes. Preserve the existing interpreter, cryptography, Git/SSH, timezone, process and util-linux tools, executable startup and baked validator. Build the actual runtime Dockerfile from the minimal public context, inspect exact baked-file hashes and package/tool versions offline without running startup or Supabase, and retain build/image identities. A fully uncached package build is needed to check the actual package resolution path.

This pins inputs, not bit-identical OCI output, supported architectures, current vulnerability freedom or release acceptance. Snapshot availability and exact package/security/notice inventory remain explicit requirements. Do not change service-image locks or upgrade any database in this slice. Primary references: [Docker digest references](https://docs.docker.com/reference/cli/docker/image/pull/), [Ubuntu snapshots](https://snapshot.ubuntu.com/), [APT snapshot support](https://documentation.ubuntu.com/server/how-to/software/snapshot-service/), reviewed 2026-10-03. The snapshot choice and image identities are already exercised by source verification; the actual controller image requires separate evidence.

The first uncached pinned runtime build succeeded, but its offline inspection exited 1 at `ssh -V`: the probe used an arbitrary numeric UID, with a missing account entry suspected rather than established from the original captured error. This failed attempt remains `.lab/runtime-verification/runtime-20261003T022933Z-1179589-0`; it grants no inspection acceptance. Correct the probe to use the image's standard unprivileged nobody account and retain command stderr on failures, then re-run actual build/inspection. No runtime image, host account or production service is changed by this correction.
The independent fresh-context runtime input critic found the initial static contract checker accepted deletion of CA construction and the APT CA setting. The builder added exact bootstrap, configuration and execution-order constraints with mutation fixtures; all 20 contract tests now pass. The original loss is retained rather than treating image digest checks alone as sufficient.

Corrected uncached build and offline inspection passed under the standard nobody account. Image `sha256:68f97cf97a5930b21cdc8300ab3617d4c3ab0f7fdbd9e76dec805268c2838e0c`; evidence `.lab/runtime-verification/runtime-20261003T023350Z-1291612-0`. The independent critic confirmed matching image/container identities, no network or mounts, dropped capabilities, exact current baked-file hashes, required tools and actual Git/SSH signature verification. Python 3.14.4, cryptography 46.0.5, Bun 1.3.14 and Docker CLI 29.8.2 were retained. All observed package download URLs use the fixed snapshot; live repository metadata for snapshot discovery remains permitted and is not represented as frozen metadata. Package inventory/hash and raw build/probe evidence are retained locally, not a public SBOM or vulnerability acceptance.

The fresh critic returned SPEC MET for immutable controller build inputs and retained packaging only. Frozen source `1426b536cf6a5e0f209087634a50bc51b55c0410002a7660241cc78ed94553d9`, evidence `.lab/portable-verification/run-20261003T023558Z-1335448-0`, passed all six stages with 1,134 Python and 296 Bun tests, zero skips and no recognized diagnostics. That snapshot precedes this ledger/compliance summary update; focused plan/document checks apply afterward. Full G0/G1, service operation, Docker-store portability, architecture support, bit-identical builds, security/distribution and release acceptance remain unproven.

## Immutable service image admission foundation

Declare before implementation: retain the existing public lock schema and full immutable index digests. Each installer/checker entry must bind a complete lower-case SHA-256 pin to a repository-qualified immutable reference matching its declared repository. Missing, contradictory or malformed references refuse before inventory/pulls; no bare-ID or mutable-tag fallback. Resolve only that exact immutable reference through native Docker inspection, require a singleton record, a complete daemon image ID and exact repository/digest association. Docker Hub aliases may normalize explicitly, but a different repository or digest substring is never proof. A daemon-resolved image ID may legitimately differ from the pinned registry index digest; preserve the declared digest in lock and upgrade intent records.

The specialist owns a shared reference/resolution module and installer/checker adoption with adversarial fixtures; a separate fresh critic judges actual read-only public-image output and refusal behavior. No service-image pull or Supabase lifecycle mutation is authorized in this slice. Available containerd inspection is actual evidence; classic-store fixtures are contract tests only, not an independent host trial. Remaining runtime launch, HBA, Studio, upgrade, recovery and probe paths require a durable migration inventory and subsequent connected changes. This foundation cannot grant whole-runtime portability, full G0 or release acceptance.

Official sources refreshed 2026-10-03: [Docker digest pull](https://docs.docker.com/reference/cli/docker/image/pull/), [image inspection](https://docs.docker.com/reference/cli/docker/image/inspect/), [Engine containerd store](https://docs.docker.com/engine/storage/containerd/), [Desktop image-store differences](https://docs.docker.com/desktop/features/containerd/). Docker Engine 29 fresh installs default to the containerd store; legacy store support and precise image identities must be verified independently, not inferred from the contributor daemon.

The distributed identity inspection command may bind the selected local Unix Docker socket into its owned probe container solely to issue public `image inspect` and daemon `info` requests. A read-only bind does not make the Docker API read-only; this is an explicitly limited command behavior, not a sandbox security guarantee. No environment/service/container inventory, credentials, pull or lifecycle call is issued by the probe. It requires an accessible local Unix endpoint and rejects remote contexts rather than inspecting a different daemon from the one selected by the host CLI. Source/probe image construction and cleanup are confined to fresh owned resources.

First actual identity probe built successfully but failed to write its report: inherited `/evidence` belongs to the unprivileged source-verification user, while the probe runs as root with all capabilities dropped and cannot bypass the directory's owner permissions. Attempt `.lab/image-identity-verification/identity-20261003T024739Z-1670319-0` exited 1 and grants no artifact acceptance. Set evidence ownership explicitly in the derived image build, retaining the runtime capability restrictions; preserve this failed attempt and rebuild. No public image proof or service behavior is accepted from its missing report.

The independent critic found a foundation spec gap after initial tests passed: installer inventory and admission treated every nonzero image-inspect result as missing and could pull after permission, daemon or unknown inspection errors. These are not evidence of image absence. Require a narrowly recognized native no-such-image diagnostic bound to the exact requested immutable reference; other failed/ambiguous inspections block and refuse before any accumulated pulls. Add red-first mixed missing/error fixtures and retain stderr. Earlier successful source/probe snapshots establish the earlier code only and do not grant the repaired slice acceptance.

Final independent verdict: SPEC MET for immutable service image admission foundation only. The reviewer independently reproduced the native permission-error loss, then verified the repair refuses before any accumulated pull. Shared lock/repository validation rejects malformed, unrelated and substring proof; fixture daemon IDs may identify either index or configuration. Repository syntax/coherence does not itself prove a future custom registry is public or available. The existing frozen candidate references are public; broader registry policy remains separate.

Final actual probe `.lab/image-identity-verification/identity-20261003T025156Z-1854442-0` passed for nine existing public images plus deliberate proof substitutions and one actual absent immutable reference. Source `fbaadaa25f721f2f28b4174801f18ca4acea36fd7f6b2e7847ed29a41ea712f9`; probe image `sha256:03a35736c27bcc3929fba4dea8e3f79b21d8f8468b7db33a644cebcbebc473af`. The critic verified native file hashes, canonical repository associations, exact image/container identities, restrictions and absence of the owned container and both fresh image tags after cleanup. No service layers were pulled or Supabase services started.

The same frozen source passed all six offline stages in `.lab/portable-verification/run-20261003T025157Z-1854709-0`, with 1,155 Python and 296 Bun tests, zero failures/skips and no recognized diagnostics. Initial source and corrected probe mechanical successes remain historical SPEC NOT MET snapshots for the subsequently found native-error gap. All evidence paths/hash summaries and losses are retained in the ledger; ignored local raw evidence is not durable public release evidence. This snapshot precedes these compliance/ledger summaries, checked separately by focused planning/document checks. Connected runtime/HBA/Studio/upgrade/recovery migration, actual classic-store and independent-host trials, full G0 and release remain unaccepted.

## Durable runtime immutable identity migration

Declare before implementation: migrate the connected durable Runtime service launch and initial database HBA admission to the shared strict repository-qualified immutable reference contract. Resolve the exact reference before any service creation/replacement, requiring complete daemon identity and exact repository proof; run by reference with `--pull=never` so launch never downloads implicitly. Keep declared lock digests as logical upgrade authorization and version markers. Retained container comparisons and HBA target checks use the resolved daemon ID. Preserve existing owner, volume, network, secrets, configuration, explicit reconciliation and one-writer generation checks, and never replace the database under a stateless upgrade authorization.

The specialist must trace launch and HBA callers before edits and update affected synthetic fixtures with valid public-format proof, without production bypasses. A separate fresh critic tests actual disposable fixture behavior and adversarial refusal, alongside all HBA/upgrade/crash source suites. Any actual Docker trial creates only freshly owned, network-disabled lightweight fixture containers from already available public images, with no Supabase pulls/services, host secret access or retained user-container changes. Whole service startup, recovery helper, Studio and standalone upgrade/recovery migration remain separate connected work, not implicitly accepted by this slice. Current official Docker digest/inspection references from the prior foundation apply; complete cross-store/independent-host operation remains unproven.

The existing Docker-only identity wrapper adds an explicit durable fixture mode. Its trusted probe creates only one uniquely named internal network and lightweight public-image service fixture, injects its own OWNER/NETWORK/private temporary directory, and filters absence-listing requests to that owner. It omits block-device IO flags inside the fixture because no host disk is mounted, retaining memory/CPU/pids limits; IO enforcement is outside this trial. Additional capability/privilege restrictions apply only to the fixture transport. Creation/reuse/refusal is exercised through actual Runtime.launch; HBA initialization and database behavior remain source/adversarial tests, not a service trial. Native stderr is retained and unexpected diagnostics refuse; all own resources are cleaned by child and outer-wrapper exact fresh names.

First actual durable fixture passed in `.lab/durable-identity-verification/identity-20261003T030214Z-2113251-0`, but root review found the fixture guard could reject an attempted unexpected mutation before logging it, and the refusal case accepted any RuntimeError. Strengthen evidence by recording attempted runtime commands before guard checks and requiring each precise production refusal reason. This earlier mechanical success does not grant final slice acceptance. The critic also found the shared wrapper's read-only comment inaccurate for durable mode; correct it before final freeze. Retain earlier source/fixture evidence as historical and re-run the strengthened fixture.

Reviewer containment incident: the fresh runtime critic imported `run_checks.source_digest()` on the host checkout three times. This helper walks the baked public image tree, and does not apply Dockerignore itself. On the host it could therefore hash ignored `.git`, `.lab` and `.secrets` contents; only aggregate hashes were output, no contents or file names. The reviewer confirmed this use and ceased it. Host hashes are invalid comparison evidence and are discarded. No additional private-file inspection is permitted to investigate contents.

Before repair, declare a fail-closed usage boundary: source_digest must refuse when its resolved source root is not the distributed image's fixed `/opt/sbarbase` root, before traversal or any file-content read. This fixed image path is a public container contract, not a workstation path dependency. Tests must demonstrate refusal before mocked traversal/read, and retain normal baked-image hash behavior. No host override or permissive environment bypass. Re-run actual fixture and six source stages after this guard. The helper is not a sandbox for arbitrary untrusted code; it prevents this accidental host invocation. Original image-only runtime evidence remains scoped historical proof, while final acceptance awaits guard verification.

The first guarded image trials both refused before execution: the declared guard used `/app`, but the authoritative verification Dockerfile has WORKDIR `/opt/sbarbase`. Root's contract chose the wrong public image path, and the static review missed that mismatch. Actual evidence `.lab/portable-verification/run-20261003T031016Z-2304032-0` and `.lab/durable-identity-verification/identity-20261003T031015Z-2303566-0` exited 1, with no source stages or fixture lifecycle accepted. Correct the guard to the actual distributed Dockerfile root and strengthen the contract test to bind that value to the Dockerfile, then rerun actual images. No host hashing is permitted.

Containment scope refinement before additional build: a fixed path alone can also be a legitimate native host checkout path, especially `/opt/sbarbase`. Require a separate fixed public-tree marker installed outside the checkout by the verification Dockerfile, in addition to the exact image root. Missing, symlinked or invalid marker must refuse before source traversal; no environment override. The marker is not private content and is created only inside the verifier image. This is accidental-use containment, not protection against arbitrary root-controlled code. Test host-like matching roots with absent marker as well as the Dockerfile root/marker binding.

Final durable Runtime verdict: SPEC MET, rebound by the independent runtime critic to public frozen source `09ddb471d86fb32d6b4bcab1a522f14bb6779b7de128b7497009475cda9469ff`. Actual fixture `.lab/durable-identity-verification/identity-20261003T031425Z-2381650-0` passed creation by qualified reference, retained reuse, database image drift refusal and owner collision refusal. Attempted-command recording and precise refusal reasons prevent the fixture guard from masquerading as production refusal. Native operations retain limits and pull-never; all own fixture container/network and source/probe tags were independently confirmed removed. HBA admission uses daemon IDs in source/adversarial tests; no actual database or Supabase service ran.

Final source `.lab/portable-verification/run-20261003T031426Z-2382005-0` matches that fixture hash and passed six stages, 1,170 Python and 296 Bun tests, zero failures/skips and no recognized diagnostics. Initial broad host discovery had seven incomplete synthetic pin errors, corrected by connected fixture updates; host SQLite/HTTPError ResourceWarnings remain historical contributor-test observations and are not accepted as release evidence. The final portable Python log contains no ResourceWarning. Existing HBA/upgrade/crash protocol schemas and logical upgrade/version digests are unchanged.

A separate fresh critic returned SPEC MET for the accidental host-use guard only. Its independent owned public image had the same aggregate source hash, actual source root `/opt/sbarbase` and root-owned 0444 external marker. Ten independent refusal controls and five containment tests passed without host helper invocation, mounts, socket or network. Evidence `.lab/source-guard-review/review.json` and `probe.log`; own resources removed. Its first inspection attempt found an already-cleaned source image and exited 125 before Python execution; it rebuilt the unchanged allowlisted public image rather than using host hashing. The earlier `/app` guard failures and host containment incident remain recorded, not hidden.

## Upgrade and rollback immutable image admission

Declare before implementation: validate every target or historical version lock entry with shared repository-qualified immutable proof before native inventory or pulls. ## Native full backup workflow and rollback

Declare before implementation: execute actual backup.create/restore and create_storage/restore_storage functions against a fresh owned PostgreSQL fixture, owned objects volume and public verifier-local state only. The bar is successful restoration of exact synthetic rows, files, DB connection limit/ACL, version 1 archive digests and metadata; actual exported snapshot must exclude a bounded writer committed after snapshot export while remaining consistent with manifest counts. Independently inject one precise native restore-tool failure after database rename and one object extraction failure after file move; each must restore the original post-backup rows/files/ACL/connection limits, leave no false success restore record and restart only fixture services. Missing required evidence, stderr warnings, leaked resources or rollback mismatch refuses scoped acceptance. Do not bypass image/owner/volume admission, real snapshot/count/archive validation or real database/file manipulation.

A bounded fixture may use separately declared service doubles for Auth/REST/Storage stop/start, endpoint publication and health transport, since this stage tests actual backup data restoration rather than the full Supabase SDK/API stack. Doubles must be explicit in evidence, with their identities/commands constrained, and must not silently count as real service health or full G2/release proof. The fixture runs only Linux Docker, already-local locked public images, unique owned names, internal network, no published ports, resource bounds and exact cleanup. Source digest only in baked verifier image with marker. Root owns ledger/harness/evidence, specialist owns workflow fixture and any genuine backup defect repair, fresh critic owns independent controls and unique per-round reports. No user project/private files/services/secrets, release, image pulls, unrelated Docker cleanup or host source hash.

Official PostgreSQL 17 logical backup and pg_restore documents reviewed 2026-10-03: https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/backup-dump.html and https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/app-pgrestore.html. Consistent single-database logical snapshots, roles/cluster scope, restore into a fresh database and explicit error handling are technical constraints; exact rollback integrity and refusal-before-success are owner criteria. A synthetic function workflow does not establish managed cloud migration, real Auth/REST/Storage readiness, physical/WAL/PITR, HA, multi-host support or release. No frozen candidate version/pin/archive protocol changes authorized in this slice.

## Restore cutover completion and resumable readiness

Declare before implementation: make environment and shared Storage post-data restore completion durable and resumable. Data-applied readiness-pending checkpoint must be privately and atomically persisted before service start/publication/health; its identity binds environment or Storage, backup/archive hashes, exact restore timestamp, admitted native database container and restored database OID. Service start/publication/health failure returns nonzero, retains checkpoint, does not claim success and never replays pg_restore, database rename/drop or file extraction. A published explicit CLI completion command re-admits image/container/volume and validates identity before only service start/publication/health. Retry may preserve newly committed rows/files and must not validate counts against the old snapshot after cutover. Changed DB OID/container, malformed or incomplete checkpoint, wrong path/environment/stamp, archive identity mismatch and unsuccessful health must refuse completion. Successful retry records completion atomically and repeated completion does not mutate data. Version 1 archives and existing logical pins stay unchanged. Tests include both environment and shared Storage startup and health failures, safe retry preserving post-cutover writes, rejection controls and evidence of no destructive retry commands.

Named reference constraints reviewed 2026-10-03: PostgreSQL 17 SQL dump https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/backup-dump.html describes snapshot replacement; Docker start https://docs.docker.com/reference/cli/docker/container/start/ starts containers without proving application readiness. Owner spec bar above adds explicit state and safe completion rather than treating process start as restore success. Current code can allow shared Storage/direct clients to write before data-phase rollback; complete write fencing is a separate unresolved gate. This slice must not claim transaction-wide rollback, crash safety for the earlier data phase, actual Supabase API readiness, full G2, HA/PITR or release. Do not put service health inside destructive rollback try.

Use a specialist builder and fresh critic. Root may extend the existing owned public native workflow fixture with explicit constrained lifecycle/health failure doubles and actual native PostgreSQL/file writes after cutover; this tests real data preservation and production completion orchestration, not real Supabase service readiness. Same unique resource names, local locked images, bounded Docker resources, internal network, no published ports or pulls/private state/user services. No host source_digest. All losses and superseded attempts remain in unique reviewer evidence.

Bar refinement before checkpoint implementation: pending completion must prevent a second restore, discard of its retained predecessors and pruning/removal of its archive until readiness is resolved. Preserve original failure semantics and explicit retry command. Source/API callers and parser branches must be traced, not inferred. Initial pre-data operation journal and complete all-writer fencing are deliberately unresolved broader safety work, not established by the readiness-only checkpoint.

Completion fixture refinement before native execution: maximum owned transient helpers expanded from 32 to 64 to accommodate four actual post-cutover data-preservation cases. Fixture must enforce that bound before creation and outer cleanup enumerates only the exact unique run helper names 1 through 64. Existing parent/DB/network/volume resource limits remain unchanged.

Independent historical packet and exact owned resource absence checks passed. Native `.lab/restore-completion-verification/identity-20261003T051751Z-648461-0` passed nine cases, 531 native commands and 32 bounded helpers. ## Fenced staged restore and explicit cutover journal

Declare before implementation: replace live-name --create data restoration with a fenced staging workflow for environment and shared Storage v1 archives. The bar is exact native preservation of schema/data ownership and grants, database owner/ACL/connection limit/comment, database and role-in-database settings, original encoding/locale, stored snapshot counts and object bytes, without silently losing metadata previously restored by --create. Unsupported archive/runtime worker properties must refuse before service/database/file mutation. Retain one target admin restore session. An admin control connection to postgres checks the stage OID and sets staging ALLOW_CONNECTIONS false before archive SQL. Replay and target validation use only the retained target connection, with no parallel or reconnect restoration/validation. Original target is ALLOW_CONNECTIONS false, all backends drained with bounded confirmation and prepared transactions refused before rename. Both original and staged databases remain fenced through SQL validation, object replacement and rename cutover. Hold a proven object writer barrier: stop shared Storage as well as selected app services, verify stop before file mutation, explicitly documenting neighboring Storage outage. Neighbor database remains usable and its data unchanged.

Durable private initial operation journal must exist before first stop/data mutation, bind immutable archive/native container and original/stage/previous identities, record each recovery-relevant phase, and guard competing restore/discard/prune. Cutover checkpoint must be durable before reopening restored database. Failures before reopening preserve or recover original database/files while fenced, retaining sanitized explicit recovery guidance on journal/recovery failures. Once reopened, preserve restored data and new writes, use existing readiness completion rather than destructive rollback. Crash/kill fault injection and explicit guarded recovery must cover original rename, staged rename, file replacement, checkpoint persistence and reopening, not rely only on source inspection. Never claim complete crash recovery until actual interruptions are verified.

SQL-only fences cannot constrain arbitrary trusted-superuser executable archive code or privileged background workers that bypass ALLOW_CONNECTIONS. Establish a documented pinned worker admission contract before mutation; reject unknown writers/preloads/active bypass configurations. Classify and test supported ordinary Auth/REST/Storage/developer/Studio/Realtime clients, held sessions and normal superuser connections, plus available known worker behavior. Do not rely on random names, revoked CONNECT, connection-limit zero or HBA alone as universal fencing. Scope remains trusted archive/admin, classified runtime; unsupported features remain explicit rather than silently ignored. Preserve version 1 archives and native strict image/owner/volume admission. Backup-time DB/object races remain a different unaccepted cross-plane capture requirement.

Official PostgreSQL 17 sources reviewed 2026-10-03: https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/app-pgrestore.html (--create includes DB ACL/settings and role settings; emitted SQL can use a retained session), https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/sql-createdatabase.html and https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/sql-alterdatabase.html (ALLOW_CONNECTIONS false versus limited connections), https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/bgworker.html (privileged background worker bypass), https://github.com/citusdata/pg_cron (libpq/background modes). These are technical constraints; staged metadata/neighbor recovery tests and durable operation state are owner acceptance requirements. Source_fence existing DB-only helper explicitly does not fence file/S3 transfers. No full Supabase/SDK, arbitrary extensions, API health, cloud migration, HA/PITR or release claim from this slice.
